FACT//DUEL · OPERATIONAL COMPLIANCE FILE

What it takes to take money

BUILD D + A. DO NOT BUILD B ANYWHERE. DO NOT BUILD C YET.

AS OF 2026-09-14REGIONS 3 ITEMS 169UNVERIFIED 130 OPEN QUESTIONS 150

This is a working file, not legal advice. No money is taken by FACT//DUEL today and no code in this repository can take any. Every figure below was researched and then checked by a second pass whose job was to refute it; figures marked UNVERIFIED survived neither and must be confirmed before they enter a budget or a geofence. Ticks are this browser’s local record and are not a legal record.

Closed, not merely hard

No filing, budget or opinion opens these. They are here first so no plan is built on one.

  • INDIA — MODEL B. Absolute and criminal. PROGA s.5 with s.2(1)(g) catching skill, chance or both; 3 years' imprisonment, Rs 1 crore, cognizable, non-bailable, directors personally liable. No sweepstakes, dual-currency or AMOE carve-out exists, and s.1(2) gives extraterritorial reach so there is no offshore escape. Head Digital Works v Union of India is pending with NO STAY — plan on the Act standing.
  • INDIA — MODEL C. Absolute and criminal, and there is no licence to buy at any price. The only statutory exit is the e-sports carve-out, which requires prior recognition under the National Sports Governance Act 2025 as a multi-sports-event discipline; a trivia app will not obtain it.
  • INDIA — COINS BUYING ENTRY TO ANY PRIZE OF VALUE, INSIDE MODEL A. Not contested, absolute. This converts A into C and lands in the criminal provision.
  • INDIA — TRANSFERABILITY, GIFTING, SECONDARY MARKETS OR ACCOUNT TRADING, IN EVERY MODEL. External convertibility defeats the permissive construction of s.2(1)(j) on its own terms.
  • INDIA — PAYMENT RAILS FOR B AND C. The payment-facilitation offence attaches to the aggregator as well as the operator, so no RBI-authorised payment aggregator will onboard at any price. Do not attempt to build one.
  • MODEL B AS A STORE-NATIVE PRODUCT, ANYWHERE. Apple 5.3.3 forbids selling stake currency through IAP at all, and Google Play bans Play Billing inside licensed gambling apps. The two policies do not compose: there is no store on which Model B is a native product.
  • MODEL B AND MODEL C ON STRIPE. Both sit in the PROHIBITED column with NO pre-approval path. Restricted categories can be pre-approved; prohibited ones cannot. Stripe's prohibited entry — 'Payments of an entry or player fee that promise the entrant or player will win a prize of value' — describes Model C verbatim.
  • PADDLE AND LEMON SQUEEZY, FOR COIN SALES IN ANY MODEL INCLUDING MODEL A. The AUP prohibits virtual currency and stored value outright.
  • MODEL B IN GERMANY, POLAND, CYPRUS, SLOVENIA, BELGIUM, AUSTRIA AND FINLAND. No licence class exists — this is closed, not merely unlicensed. Finland is closed until 1 July 2027.
  • FRANCE, FOR THE CURRENT PAID FORMAT. L320-1 CSI bites wherever ANY part of the gain is due to chance, and the random question draw plus reflex timers supply it. France does not prohibit skill contests as a class, but there is no ANJ licence class to apply for. Geo-block hard — IP plus payment BIN plus device signals.
  • RANDOMISED PAID REWARDS (LOOT BOXES, GACHA) ANYWHERE IN THE EU. Belgian criminal exposure. This one is a design rule, not a market exclusion: do not ship the mechanic at all in the EU build.
  • AN MGA LICENCE AS A ROUTE TO OTHER EU MARKETS. It does not passport, and C-440/23 (European Lotto and Betting Ltd, judgment 16 April 2026) confirms member states may prohibit gambling licensed elsewhere and attach civil consequences including nullity and restitution. Separately, filing for MGA Type 3 is self-defeating: it is by definition a class of GAMES OF CHANCE, so filing concedes the point and destroys the skill argument in Germany and Austria.
  • MODEL B NATIONALLY IN THE USA, AT SEED STAGE. Not because licensure is theoretically unavailable — Nevada NRS 463.160 and NJ DGE esports licensing exist — but because no state has an off-the-shelf licence category fitting a peer-to-peer mobile trivia app at viable cost or lead time, ~10 states criminalise skill wagering outright, ~10 more have express dual-currency bans reaching your processors and vendors, and state MTL runs 3-12 months per state with NY and CA at 12-18+ months. The commercial blocker arrives before the legal one: no PSP, geolocation vendor or affiliate network will onboard a product whose vendors carry criminal liability under CA s.337o, NY s.912 and OK S.B. 1589.
  • EFFECTIVELY CLOSED ON REPORTED FIGURES — ITALY AND ROMANIA for an early-stage footprint. Italy's reported ~EUR 7m per-concession fee under D.Lgs. 41/2024 plus the Decreto Dignita near-total advertising ban; Romania's reported EUR 480,000 minimum authorisation tax. BOTH FIGURES ARE UNVERIFIED and Romania's is reported elsewhere at other numbers — so this is 'closed on the reported economics', not a verified closure. Do not spend to verify unless the strategy changes.
  • NOT CLOSED, CONTRARY TO THE UNCORRECTED BRIEFS — flagged so you are not misled in the other direction: US Model C is unsettled and arguable, not barred; India Model A's purchased-coin duel variant is CONTESTED, not criminal; Montana does not belong on the skill-wagering list (the cite was fabricated); and Ireland's remote gaming B2C licences are reportedly not yet open (Q4 2026 or 2027-28) rather than unavailable in principle.

The decision matrix

Four candidate models against three regions. Colour is status; a model carries a letter and never a colour.

Legality of each candidate model in each region
ModelUnited StatesEuropean UnionIndia
A Closed-loop cosmetic

Buy coins; coins buy cosmetics only; never convert back to money or a prize of value; nothing staked player-v-player.

 CLEAR

No prize of value means no lottery and no 'bet or wager' under 31 U.S.C. s.5362(1)(A); coins are not convertible virtual currency under FIN-2019-G001 s.1.3, and a closed-loop arrangement under $2,000/device/day is not a prepaid program. Two structural traps, both architectural: WA RCW 9.46.0285 plus Kater v. Churchill Downs, 886 F.3d 784 (9th Cir. 2018) and NY Penal Law s.225.00(6) treat a credit extending 'the privilege of playing' as a thing of value, so a PURCHASED coin that is staked and can be lost satisfies both consideration and prize with zero cash-out — purchased coins must therefore be structurally incapable of being staked. Note s.5362(1)(E)(viii) has been struck from the authority line: it shelters only free credits and stops applying the moment coins are sold. Also engaged: unclaimed-property/escheat duties on unredeemed balances (thresholds and dormancy periods entirely UNVERIFIED), non-18 ages of majority in AL/NE/MS (UNVERIFIED), COPPA, and FTC dark-pattern exposure on multi-step currency conversion (Epic $245m; Cognosphere $20m).

 CLEAR

Lawful but CONDITIONAL, not flat. Closed-loop coins fail the prize limb of every EU-27 gambling definition and the stake limb of the AMLR. On e-money, the cliff edge is THIRD-PARTY ACCEPTANCE AND TRANSFERABILITY, not redeemability: EMD2 Art 2(2) limb (ii) requires acceptance by a person other than the issuer, and EBA Q&A 2022_6336 (Final, 17/01/2025, citing CJEU C-661/22) reads it as requiring transferability and voluntary acceptance as a separate monetary asset. So protect NON-TRANSFERABILITY, not 'no refunds' — the earlier instruction to avoid goodwill cash refunds and account-closure balance returns is removed as actively harmful. Four named conditions: what 'entries' buy; NO randomised paid rewards (Belgium criminal exposure); the CPC virtual-currency principles; and DSA/JuSchG minor protection. Owes destination VAT via non-Union OSS from the first euro, CRD Arts 6/8(2)/9, DCD Art 19 and Rome I Art 6 mandatory consumer law regardless.

 CLEAR

Lawful in its pure form — money in, cosmetics out sits inside 'online social game' at PROGA s.2(1)(i), and that is the ONLY lawful route to taking money for a duel-shaped product in India. Two absolute limits inside A: coins buying entry to any PRIZE OF VALUE is unlawful, not contested — that converts A into C; and transferability, gifting, secondary markets or account trading defeat the permissive reading on its own terms. One genuinely CONTESTED variant: whether purchased, NON-CASHABLE coins staked player-v-player are themselves an 'online money game' is untested — no OGAI determination, no judgment, and the weight of published 2026 Indian commentary is on the permissive side (including the source the original brief cited only for a soft line while suppressing its actual conclusion). The corrected lane adopts the conjunctive reading of s.2(1)(j) (convertible-to-money AND purchased) and notes s.2(1)(g) reads 'monetary AND other enrichment', not 'or'. The prior assertion of settled criminality is WITHDRAWN: the two-ledger architecture is now a risk-buyout, not a legal requirement. A written Indian counsel opinion on s.2(1)(j) is a hard gate before any India purchase flow, because the exposure is criminal and unquantified rather than a fine.

B Dual currency / sweepstakes

Buy coins; stake them player-v-player; winnings redeemable for cash or prizes of value.

 CLOSED

Unlawful as designed with no commercially viable licensed path. About ten states criminalise wagering on contests of SKILL by statute or judicial construction regardless of the skill/chance debate (AZ, AR, CA, DE, FL, IL, LA, NV, SC, SD, plus TN and WA via near-any-chance and material-element tests; MONTANA IS REMOVED — the cite the original brief used is withdrawn as fabricated). Roughly ten states enacted express dual-currency 'online sweepstakes' prohibitions in 2025-26 reaching payment processors, geolocation vendors and media affiliates as well as the operator (CA, CT, IN, LA, ME, MT, NJ, NY, OK, TN), with two more (IA, NV) granting open-ended enforcement authority instead of defining the offence. Be precise about why: licensure is NOT unavailable in principle — Nevada NRS 463.160 and NJ DGE esports event wagering exist — the accurate claim is that no state has an off-the-shelf licence category fitting a peer-to-peer mobile trivia app at viable cost or lead time. Federally, FinCEN MSB registration is mandatory (Form 107 within 180 days, AML programme, SARs) and the wagering excise tax is genuinely adverse because stake money alone comprises the prize. Commercially blocked before it is legally blocked: no PSP, geolocation vendor or affiliate will onboard a product whose vendors carry criminal/civil liability under CA s.337o, NY s.912 and OK S.B. 1589.

 CLOSED

Not launchable as a pan-EU proposition. There is no EU gambling licence and no EU gambling regulator — 27 separate regimes. In Germany, Poland, Cyprus, Slovenia, Belgium, Austria and Finland NO LICENCE CLASS EXISTS: this is closed, not merely unlicensed (Finland closed until 1 July 2027). Licence-required in a minority (Malta, and depending on structure DK/SE/ES/IE/IT), at fees and lead times that are UNVERIFIED for every member state except Malta. No member state accepts a dual paid-plus-free entry route as cleansing the paid route. Player-v-player staking of purchased coins IS the transfer of value to a person other than the issuer, so EMI authorisation or a licensed partner as issuer/merchant of record is mandatory, compounded by PSD3 repealing EMD2 with mandatory re-application within 24-30 months. AML obligations are LIVE TODAY under national AMLD4/5 transpositions — NOT from 10 July 2027 — with CDD at EUR 2,000 on wagering or collection of winnings; a launch built to the 2027 date would be unlawful on day one in most member states.

 CLOSED

Criminal on every reading, and absolutely closed. PROGA s.5 (in force 1 May 2026) criminalises offering an 'online money game' — s.2(1)(g), 'irrespective of whether such game is based on skill, chance, or both' — with 3 years' imprisonment, a Rs 1 crore fine, cognizable and non-bailable, directors personally liable. Cash or prize redemption supplies monetary enrichment, so s.2(1)(g) is satisfied however you read it. There is no sweepstakes, dual-currency or AMOE carve-out in PROGA, and s.1(2) gives express extraterritorial reach, so there is no offshore escape. On payments, the facilitation offence attaches to the aggregator as well as the operator, so no RBI-authorised payment aggregator will onboard at any price. Plan on the Act standing: Head Digital Works v Union of India, T.C.(C) 133/2025 is before a three-judge bench, listed 5 Aug 2026, no judgment as at 14 Sep 2026 and NO STAY.

C Paid-entry skill contest

Fixed entry fee; prize pool fixed, announced in advance and operator-funded, not dependent on entrant count.

 UNSETTLED

The strongest real-money structure and the only fundable one, and materially safer than the original federal brief said — but not cleared. The excise-tax position REVERSED in C's favour: AM 2020-009 endorses Rev. Rul. 57-521 (an entry fee in a contest of mental skill with a guaranteed operator-funded prize is not a wager unless the entry fees alone comprise the prize), so the 'under-appreciated killer' framing is withdrawn for C and re-aimed at B. UIGEA s.5362(1)(E)(ix) does not shelter trivia (condition (II) requires real-world statistical outcomes). Money transmission turns on receiving money FOR TRANSMISSION TO ANOTHER PERSON, so an operator-funded, entrant-count-independent prize frequently does NOT trigger state MTL — the flat '49 states plus DC' claim materially overstated the burden for the design being recommended. Still barred by a hard core of states (AZ, AR, DE, FL, LA, NV, SC, SD, TN, WA, plus CT/IL conservative, plus MT unresolved), so roughly 11 states geofenced plus a per-state opinion. White v. Cuomo is a NY constitutional design heuristic, not clearance. Conditions for the defensible version: prize operator-funded, fixed and announced in advance, no withdrawable balance, no chance elements on the paid path (no randomised question draw, no matchmaking variance), and every opponent a disclosed human or disclosed bot. Payments are the practical blocker: Stripe names this structure in its PROHIBITED column verbatim.

 UNSETTLED

Genuinely unsettled and the split does NOT run where the original brief drew it. Camp 1 (outside gambling law if chance does not predominate): Germany and Austria only — but in Germany escaping GlueStV s.3(1) lands you in s.33d GewO, which requires its own trade Erlaubnis with a BKA Unbedenklichkeitsbescheinigung; whether s.33d reaches a purely online offering is CONTESTED and is the single largest open question in the EU lane. Netherlands is contested-adverse (appellate authority runs against skill claims). Camp 2 (LICENCE-REQUIRED): Sweden — REVERSED from the brief, Spellagen's kombinationsspel expressly covers skill-plus-chance and the Spelinspektionen carve-out requires a competition decided WITHOUT any chance element, which a random question draw defeats — plus Ireland, Italy, Spain, Denmark and Malta. France is caught because L320-1 CSI bites wherever ANY part of the gain is due to chance; France does not prohibit skill contests as a class, but the random draw and reflex timers almost certainly put FACT//DUEL inside, and there is no ANJ licence class to apply for. Separately unsettled at EU level: whether a 5/7/10-second timed duel over a randomly drawn set is a 'game of chance with an element of skill' under AMLD4 Art 3(14) — that single characterisation decides gambling licensing, AML obliged-entity status AND VAT Art 135(1)(i) exemption simultaneously. Filing for an MGA Type 3 licence would concede the product is a game of chance and destroy the skill argument in DE/AT; Type 3 and Type 4 are not interchangeable.

 CLOSED

Criminal, and there is no licence to buy. A fixed entry fee is 'paying fees' and an announced cash prize is monetary enrichment, so s.2(1)(g) is satisfied. The only statutory exit is the e-sports carve-out, which requires prior recognition under the National Sports Governance Act 2025 as a multi-sports-event discipline — a trivia app will not obtain it. Same penalties as B: 3 years, Rs 1 crore, cognizable, non-bailable, directors personally liable, extraterritorial under s.1(2). This is the bluntest cell in the matrix: unlike US Model C, which is unsettled and arguable, India Model C has no path at all. The appeal route, if OGAI moves against you, is to the Secretary MeitY as Appellate Authority (30 days to file, 30 days for disposal) then High Court writ — correcting the earlier claim that the Rules dropped the appellate tier, which was fabricated.

D Subscription

Recurring fee for features and cosmetics. No stake, no prize of value.

 CLEAR

No gambling statute is engaged federally or in any state. Burden is consumer-protection only: ROSCA applies; the FTC click-to-cancel rule was vacated in Custom Communications, Inc. v. FTC, No. 24-3137 (8th Cir. 8 Jul 2025), BUT that restored the 1973 Prenotification Negative Option Rule still in force at 16 C.F.R. Part 425 (the 'no FTC negative option rule' claim is withdrawn), and an ANPRM published 11 Mar 2026. State auto-renewal laws impose click-to-cancel-equivalent duties as binding law, not prudence: California ARL/AB 2863 in force since 1 Jul 2025, Virginia 1 Jul 2026, NYC 1 Oct 2026. Newly added: unclaimed-property/escheat exposure and non-18 ages of majority. COPPA applies if any subscriber could be under 13.

 CLEAR

Lawful and needs no gambling licence in any of the 27 — CONDITIONAL on the subscription conferring no competitive advantage in any prize contest. The MGA counts a periodic subscription as consideration where it advantages the player, so any competitive advantage bought by a subscription converts D into C. No stake, no prize, no transferable stored value, so outside the EMD2 perimeter entirely. Burden is VAT (destination, non-Union OSS), CRD Arts 6/8(2)/9, and national auto-renewal rules. Newly flagged: DCD Art 19 constrains mid-term changes, Directive (EU) 2020/1828 makes a bad cancellation flow a collective-redress target, and the European Accessibility Act applies once you exit microenterprise status.

 CLEAR

Expressly blessed. PROGA s.2(1)(i)(ii) permits a subscription or one-time access fee 'provided that such payment is not in the nature of a stake or wager'. This is the cleanest cell in the entire twelve. Obligations that still attach: 18% GST, with OIDAR registration from the first Indian rupee if supplied from outside India; DPDP Act 2023 s.9 child-data duties (substantive obligations bite 13 May 2027, and note s.9(3) is NOT absolute — s.9(4) permits Government exemptions, and an 18+ gate creates a verification obligation rather than removing one); and the 2026 Rules' affirmative user-safety duties on online social games (age verification, parental controls, time limits, fair-play monitoring, grievance redressal) — asserted by the audit but UNVERIFIED as to scope, and a real unmapped cost.

Findings that change the plan

  • THE A/D PATH HAS NO APPROVAL LEAD TIME IN ANY OF THE THREE REGIONS. No licence, no regulator, no application, no bond, no capital requirement. First revenue is gated by your engineering estimate and app-store review, not by law. Every multi-month and multi-year delay in this document belongs to B or C and is imported voluntarily.
  • MODEL B FAILS THREE INDEPENDENT GATES, EACH SUFFICIENT ON ITS OWN: it is criminal in India with extraterritorial reach; it is unlawful in ~10 US states that criminalise skill wagering plus ~10 more with express dual-currency bans; and it is unbankable because Stripe lists it PROHIBITED with no pre-approval path while Apple 5.3.3 forbids selling stake currency through IAP at all. You cannot fix one and proceed.
  • MODEL C IS AS UNBANKABLE AS MODEL B ON MAINSTREAM RAILS. Stripe's PROHIBITED column contains 'Payments of an entry or player fee that promise the entrant or player will win a prize of value' — a verbatim description of Model C. The Adyen 'LEGAL gambling or Game of Skill' escape hatch may be illusory, because the licence it appears to require does not exist for pure skill contests in the UK. One written question to Adyen underwriting settles it, and it must be asked BEFORE any Model C engineering.
  • THE PAYMENTS REFUSAL IS STATUTORY, NOT NEGOTIABLE RISK APPETITE. UIGEA (31 U.S.C. ss.5361-5367), implemented at 12 CFR Part 233 and 31 CFR Part 132, requires ACH, card, cheque, wire and money-transmitting systems to BLOCK restricted transactions, and 31 U.S.C. s.5367 exposes the processor itself. Its corollary matters: UIGEA reaches only UNLAWFUL gambling, which makes the state skill-versus-chance opinion a payments PREREQUISITE, not a parallel workstream — the geo-blocking map is an output of the legality analysis.
  • THE EXCISE-TAX POSITION REVERSED IN MODEL C's FAVOUR AND AGAINST MODEL B. AM 2020-009 endorses Rev. Rul. 57-521: an entry fee in a contest of mental skill with a guaranteed operator-funded prize is not a wager UNLESS the entry fees alone comprise the prize. The 'under-appreciated killer' framing is withdrawn for C and re-aimed at B. This changes the economics of the C-versus-B decision. Caveat: Rev. Rul. 57-521's original text is known only through the quotation inside AM 2020-009 and must be pulled before it is relied on.
  • GERMANY s.33d GewO WAS THE BIGGEST OMISSION, IN THE MARKET THE ORIGINAL BRIEF SAID TO LAUNCH IN FIRST. Escaping GlueStV s.3(1) on skill predominance does not escape German permission — s.33d GewO requires a trade Erlaubnis for commercially operating other games with a chance to win, with a BKA clearance certificate as a precondition. 'Run Model C in Germany unlicensed' is WITHDRAWN.
  • SWEDEN REVERSED FROM 'OUTSIDE GAMBLING LAW' TO 'LICENCE-REQUIRED'. Spellagen's kombinationsspel expressly covers skill-plus-chance games, and the Spelinspektionen carve-out requires a competition decided WITHOUT any chance element — not merely skill-predominant. A random question draw defeats it. A founder reading the uncorrected headline would have launched paid contests in Sweden unlicensed.
  • EU AML IS LIVE TODAY, NOT FROM 10 JULY 2027. AMLD4 Art 2(1)(3)(f) has made gambling-service providers obliged entities EU-wide since the 26 June 2017 transposition deadline, and Art 3(14) already carries the 'games of chance... including those with an element of skill' definition. Building Model B to a 2027 start date would have been unlawful on day one in most member states. The escape hatch is AMLD4 Art 2(2), under which member states may exempt certain low-risk gambling providers — which also destroys the assumption that a regulation makes this uniform.
  • ONE CHARACTERISATION DECIDES THREE THINGS AT ONCE IN THE EU. Whether a 5/7/10-second timed duel over a randomly drawn question set is a 'game of chance with an element of skill' simultaneously decides gambling licensing, AML obliged-entity status and VAT Art 135(1)(i) exemption. Instruct member-state counsel on all three in ONE instruction, or you will get three inconsistent answers.
  • THE RAKE DECISION IS A BIGGER LEVER THAN THE TIMER AND MUST BE FROZEN BEFORE COUNSEL IS INSTRUCTED. Whether you take a commission from a player-funded pot or pay a fixed, announced, operator-funded prize pool is the difference between licensable and out-of-scope in Germany, Austria and the Netherlands, and it drives the US money-transmission analysis. Instruct counsel before freezing it and every opinion answers the wrong question.
  • THE 300x RULE WAS POINTED THE WRONG WAY AND THE ERROR WAS ONE-DIRECTIONAL AND EXPENSIVE. It gates REPORTING, not WITHHOLDING. Per the Instructions for W-2G and 5754 (rev. 01/2026), 24% withholding applies when winnings minus wager exceed $5,000 for sweepstakes, wagering pools and lotteries; the 300x qualifier attaches only to the trailing sports-wagering clause (IRC 3402(q)(3)). A pot funded by losers' stakes is plausibly a wagering pool. Rule: withhold 24% over $5,000 of proceeds regardless of multiple — the money is unrecoverable from the player after payment.
  • US STATE MONEY TRANSMITTER LICENSING WAS OVERSTATED FOR THE DESIGN BEING RECOMMENDED. MTL turns on receiving money FOR TRANSMISSION TO ANOTHER PERSON. Model B squarely raises it; Model C funded from the operator's own treasury with entrant-count-independent prizes frequently does not. The flat '49 states plus DC, the single largest cost and time line item' claim does not hold for C. It does hold for B, where 3-12 months per state and 12-18+ months for NY and CA make the model unfinanceable at seed.
  • INDIA MODEL A's DUEL VARIANT IS CONTESTED, NOT CRIMINAL — AND THAT CHANGES THE PRODUCT DECISION. The prior claim that purchased non-cashable coins are automatically 'other stakes' and that the escrow duel flips to criminal at the first rupee is WITHDRAWN. The two-ledger architecture is now a risk-buyout, not a legal requirement. It is still worth building, because it is also what WA and NY require in the US.
  • LICENSURE IS NOT UNAVAILABLE IN PRINCIPLE IN THE US — DO NOT BE MISLED BY THE ABSOLUTE. Nevada NRS 463.160 and NJ DGE esports event wagering licensing exist. The accurate and still-fatal claim is that no state has an off-the-shelf licence CATEGORY fitting a peer-to-peer mobile trivia app at commercially viable cost and lead time.
  • MALTA: A REPEALED INSTRUMENT WAS BEING CITED AS THE LIVE REGIME. S.L. 438.11 (Skill Games Regulations) was repealed by Act XVI of 2018; the live regime is the Gaming Act Cap. 583 plus subsidiary legislation plus MGA Directive 6 of 2018. Separately, the Malta gaming tax changes on 1 October 2026 (15% Type 1, 10% Types 2-4, device levy abolished) and — a fact missed entirely — for distance gaming the taxability test shifts from the player's PHYSICAL PRESENCE in Malta to established/permanent address or usual residence.

Bears on the free product you already ship

These do not wait for a payment surface.

  • COPPA IS IN FULL FORCE NOW, NOT PENDING — the amended Rule's compliance date passed 22 April 2026. A trivia product with cartoon-adjacent presentation, no accounts and no age gate is a plausible FTC target on child-directedness alone, entirely independently of anything to do with money. This is live exposure on today's free build and applies to all four models.
  • THE CURRENT FREE PRODUCT IS NOT UNREGULATED IN INDIA. Two regimes attach to a free product with Indian users: (a) DPDP Act 2023 s.9 child-data duties, under which the existing cohort, retention and session analytics are unlawful as applied to Indian minors (substantive obligations bite 13 May 2027; penalties up to Rs 200 crore for s.9 breaches and Rs 250 crore for security-safeguard failures); and (b) the 2026 Rules' social-game safety duties — age verification, parental controls, time limits, fair-play monitoring, grievance redressal — which are reported to apply to online social games generally, i.e. to the live free build. UNVERIFIED as to scope; could not be corroborated from SCC Online, Bar & Bench, Legal500 or ipleaders. Do not defer India work until monetisation.
  • SIMULATED COINS AS A 'THING OF VALUE': THE TRAP IS ONE CODE CHANGE AWAY, NOT ACTIVE TODAY. WA RCW 9.46.0285 and NY Penal Law s.225.00(6) define a thing of value to include a credit involving 'extension of a service, entertainment or a privilege of playing at a game or scheme without charge', and Kater v. Churchill Downs, 886 F.3d 784 (9th Cir. 28 Mar 2018) held Big Fish Casino's virtual chips were a thing of value on exactly that basis. TODAY coins are minted free and cannot be bought, so the consideration element fails and no gambling offence is engaged. The moment a coin is SOLD, a purchased coin that is staked and can be lost satisfies BOTH consideration and prize in WA and NY with zero cash-out. Kater-line claims settled for a reported $155m. This is the single strongest reason the two-ledger split (purchased = spend-only and unstakeable; earned = stakeable and worthless) must be designed in before the first purchase flow, not retrofitted.
  • THE FREE PRODUCT'S FEDERAL SAFE HARBOUR DOES NOT TRANSFER TO THE PAID ONE. UIGEA s.5362(1)(E)(viii) shelters participation where players stake only 'points or credits that the sponsor of the game or contest provides to participants FREE OF CHARGE' — that covers today's demo coin exactly, and stops applying the moment coins are sold. It has been struck from Model A's authority line; paid Model A rests on s.5362(1)(A) alone.
  • BOTS: THE PRODUCT'S CURRENT DISCLOSURE POSTURE IS GOOD AND MUST NOT REGRESS. FACT//DUEL ships a bot opponent (lib/server/room-engine.mjs:109, seat name 'Lucky Guess · BOT'), and the UI already discloses it in at least three places — a 'BOT' badge and bot icon in app/rivalry-widgets.tsx:78-82, a 'VS BOT' mode label at app/rivalry-widgets.tsx:200, and an explicit statement at app/screens/room-screen.tsx:136: 'Lucky Guess is ready. Its choices and response times are random.' That is the right posture and it is worth preserving deliberately, because it is the exact opposite of the conduct found in Skillz Platform Inc. v. Papaya Gaming, Ltd., No. 1:24-cv-01646 (S.D.N.Y., Cote J.) — marketing real-money head-to-head 'skill' tournaments while secretly filling them with bots.
  • THE SKILLZ v. PAPAYA EXPOSURE WAS MISSTATED IN BOTH SOURCE AND SIZE, BUT IS REAL. It is a COMPETITOR suit under the Lanham Act and New York GBL — not FTC Act s.5 (no private right of action) and not COPPA; the FTC was not a party. An April 2026 jury returned $420m; the 28 July 2026 judgment awarded $719m in disgorgement as an ALTERNATIVE remedy in lieu of the verdict, not in addition to it. It is a district-court judgment, appealable, NOT PRECEDENT, and binds no court; whether it has been appealed is UNVERIFIED. What survives every correction: undisclosed bot opponents in a PAID duel is the most dangerous fact pattern in this document, a competitor can bring it, and whatever fills a duel must be audited and disclosed before a single dollar is staked.
  • XP ASYMMETRY BETWEEN BOT AND HUMAN ROUNDS IS ITSELF A DISCLOSURE SURFACE. lib/progression.mjs pays roundCorrectBot (20) versus roundCorrectHuman and applies a humanMultiplier (lines 20, 1176, 1214, 1243), and counts humanMatches separately. That is fine and arguably helpful, but it means the system already knows and rewards the difference — so any marketing copy describing matches as 'head-to-head' without qualifying bot matches is a Lanham Act / GBL fact pattern even before money moves.
  • THE 'WINNER TAKES THE ESCROW' CLAIM ABOUT THE LIVE UI IS FALSE — verified by grep. The word 'escrow' appears in zero files under app/ and components/; it lives only in lib/server/room-engine.mjs and the test files (tests/duel.test.mjs, tests/duel-memory.test.mjs, tests/bot.test.mjs, tests/stress.mjs). The renaming work is cheap internal hygiene, not an emergency, and it was fabricated in the original brief.
  • THE EXPEDITION MECHANIC IS THE WORST-CASE FACT PATTERN UNDER EVERY CHANCE TEST — harmlessly so today. Staking at three confidence tiers on rotating question sets, plus the 5/7/10-second timer, randomised question draws and matchmaking variance, is exactly what a material-element or any-chance test bites on. With no consideration it engages nothing today. It means the paid path needs a SEPARATE contest engine with identical question sets and no variance — you cannot monetise the existing Expedition loop directly.
  • THE ARCHITECTURE ITSELF IS THE GATING DEPENDENCY. FACT//DUEL is local-only IndexedDB with no accounts. Geofencing, KYC, age gating, MTL/BSA, EU VAT location evidence (two non-contradictory pieces per sale), the last-activity ledger that state escheat requires, and the FIFO coin-lot ledger needed for the EU refund path and to prevent a stake-then-refund free-roll all require server-authoritative identity and balances. None of it retrofits onto a balance-only ledger. Separate the money ledger from the game ledger in the data model before anything else — cheapest now, very expensive later.
  • DO NOT PUBLISH THESE COMPLIANCE DOCUMENTS AT A PUBLIC URL UNDER THE COMPANY DOMAIN. This is a live risk given the pending task to build and deploy a compliance checklist page: the lane files at /home/user/fact-duel/docs/money/lanes/ contain candid adverse legal assessments, UNVERIFIED flags and explicit statements about what is unlawful. A public compliance page must be separately drafted for publication, not generated from these.
  • AGE-OF-MAJORITY AND AGE-GATE FACTS THAT BITE THE FREE PRODUCT: Alabama and Nebraska 19, Mississippi 21 (stated from general knowledge, statutes NOT read — UNVERIFIED). Under DPDP an 18+ self-declaration is NOT an exit: the Rules require due diligence that an adult claimant is not a child. Practical answer for any money model: hard-block under-18s entirely and document it, because state minimum contest ages make under-18 money play indefensible regardless of COPPA.
  • THE FTC's 25 FEB 2026 AGE-VERIFICATION ENFORCEMENT POLICY STATEMENT: existence confirmed, terms NOT. The under-18 hard block design depends on its conditions and scope. Do not design the age gate around assumed terms.

The common gate

Everything that must exist before any region-specific filing. It is the top of the prerequisite graph.

AUDIT ADDITION — the 'Adyen route' for Model C may be ILLUSORY rather than merely slow. Adyen's category word is 'Legal gambling', which implies a licensed operator. But in the UK a paid-entry prize competition decided by genuine skill, knowledge or judgement sits OUTSIDE the Gambling Commission's licensing regime entirely — there is no skill-contest licence to obtain (secondary: UK practitioner commentary on Gambling Act 2005 prize competitions; not verified against Gambling Commission primary text). If Adyen's underwriting requires a licence the applicant cannot obtain because no such licence category exists, the waiver prerequisite is unsatisfiable and Model C has no mainstream EU/UK rail either. UNVERIFIED and decision-critical: put this question to Adyen underwriting in writing BEFORE building any Model C checkout — 'what evidence of lawfulness do you accept for a paid-entry skill contest in a jurisdiction that issues no licence for it?'

It also creates a fresh escheatment obligation on the unredeemed card. Treat it as a convenience, not a legal structure.

Stage 2 — Licences, registrations, notifications 12

  • Google Play: complete the gambling-app application and hold a licence per country/state for Model B

    ISSUER
    Google Play
    COST
    UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    UNVERIFIED
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST Approved governmental operator status or registration as a licensed operator with the appropriate gambling authority, plus a valid operating licence in each specified country

    Play's Real-Money Gambling, Games, and Contests policy requires the app be free to download, NOT use Google Play In-app Billing, prevent under-age users, geo-block countries/states not covered by the licence, be rated AO (Adult Only) or IARC equivalent, and display responsible-gambling information. Unlicensed apps that let users 'wager, stake, or participate using real money... to obtain a prize of real world monetary value' are prohibited outright. Secondary reporting (Lexology, igamingexpert 2025) indicates Google tightened its treatment of sweepstakes and dual-currency models and now treats virtual currencies or items with real-world value as gambling.

  • FinCEN MSB registration (Form 107) as a money transmitter

    ISSUER
    FinCEN (US Treasury)
    COST
    UNVERIFIED (registration itself is not fee-bearing; the AML programme cost is the real cost)
    LEAD TIME
    Registration due within 180 days of the date the business is established; renewal every two years — UNVERIFIED, confirm against 31 CFR 1022.380
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST Legal entity; written AML programme; designated compliance officer

    AUDIT CORRECTION — the brief rested the whole US money-transmission analysis on FinCEN guidance FIN-2013-G001 (18 March 2013) alone. The CURRENT consolidated authority is FIN-2019-G001, 'Application of FinCEN's Regulations to Certain Business Models Involving Convertible Virtual Currencies', issued 9 May 2019, which by its own terms 'consolidates current FinCEN regulations, and related administrative rulings and guidance issued since 2011, and then applies these rules and interpretations to obligations under the BSA as they relate to current and emerging business models' — a sweep that includes FIN-2013-G001 and addresses business models the 2013 guidance does not. Point counsel at the 2019 guidance FIRST; cite 2013 only as the origin of the definitions. The 2013 definitions themselves were checked verbatim against the FinCEN page and remain accurate: 'convertible virtual currency' is virtual currency that 'either has an equivalent value in real currency, or acts as a substitute for real currency'; an 'administrator' is 'a person engaged as a business in issuing (putting into circulation) a virtual currency, and who has the authority to redeem (to withdraw from circulation) such virtual currency', and an administrator of a CVC is a money transmitter. The moment FACT//DUEL coins are redeemable for cash, the coins become CVC and the company becomes an administrator. Conversely: 'A user who obtains convertible virtual currency and uses it to purchase real or virtual goods or services is not an MSB' — which is exactly Model A, where the coin has no real-currency equivalent and cannot substitute for currency. Because this is the single most consequential legal determination in the lane, do not let a Model A clearance rest on 2013 guidance alone.

  • SAR filing capability

    ISSUER
    FinCEN
    COST
    $0 filing fee; staffing cost UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    Must file no later than 30 calendar days after initial detection
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST AML programme; transaction monitoring

    31 CFR 1022.320: an MSB must report a suspicious transaction that 'involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000'; file 'no later than 30 calendar days after the date of the initial detection'; and 'maintain a copy of any SAR filed and the original or business record equivalent of any supporting documentation for a period of five years from the date of filing the SAR'. Note the $2,000 SAR floor is far below the $10,000 CTR floor — SARs, not CTRs, will be the operative burden for a consumer game.

  • CTR filing capability for cash-equivalent aggregation over $10,000/day

    ISSUER
    FinCEN
    COST
    $0 filing fee
    LEAD TIME
    UNVERIFIED (confirm the 15-day deadline against 31 CFR 1010.306)
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST AML programme

    UNVERIFIED — 31 CFR 1010.311/1010.306 were not fetched in this research (eCFR blocked). The $10,000 currency-transaction threshold is well established but confirm applicability: CTRs attach to CURRENCY, and a card-funded online game may have little or no currency activity. Do not build CTR machinery before counsel confirms it is in scope.

  • State money transmitter licences (MTLs)

    ISSUER
    State banking departments via NMLS; model law is the CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act
    COST
    UNVERIFIED per state — surety bond, minimum net worth and permissible-investment requirements apply under the MTMA framework
    LEAD TIME
    UNVERIFIED — historically many months to years for a 40+ state footprint
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST FinCEN registration; audited financials; surety bonds; fingerprinting of control persons

    The MTMA is enacted in whole or part in 31 states per CSBS (secondary). Two carve-outs matter enormously here: the MTMA defines 'money' to EXCLUDE virtual currency, and the virtual-currency definition itself excludes certain merchant-rewards value and game-platform value (secondary — Virginia MTMA analysis; Virginia's Act is effective 1 July 2026). Only North Dakota and Minnesota have enacted the optional virtual-currency provisions (secondary). UNVERIFIED: the exact MTMA statutory text of the game-platform exclusion was not fetched. This carve-out is the single most valuable thing for counsel to confirm, because if a non-redeemable game coin sits inside it, Model A is unlicensable-free in every MTMA state and Model B's exposure turns entirely on the redeemability.

  • W-9 collection from US payees and W-8BEN from non-US payees before any payout

    ISSUER
    IRS
    COST
    $0
    LEAD TIME
    Engineering only
    APPLIES TO
    B, C

    NEEDS FIRST Payout flow

    Collect the TIN BEFORE the money moves, not at year end. Missing TIN triggers 24% backup withholding, which you cannot recover from the user after the fact.

  • Form 1099-MISC for non-wagering contest prizes

    ISSUER
    IRS
    COST
    $0 filing; filing-agent cost UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    Annual
    APPLIES TO
    C (and B if structured as a non-wagering sweepstakes)

    NEEDS FIRST W-9 on file

    Under the One Big Beautiful Bill Act the 1099-MISC reporting threshold rises from $600 to $2,000 for payments made from 2026, inflation-indexed from 2027 (secondary: Avalara, OnPay, Patriot Software — the statutory section was not fetched). Prizes and awards from a non-wagering skill contest go in Box 3 'Other income', not on Form W-2G. The IRS Instructions for Forms W-2G and 5754 (rev. 01/2026) cover gambling winnings only and do not address skill contests — confirm the characterisation with tax counsel, because it turns on whether the entry fee is a 'wager'.

  • Form W-2G for wagering winnings

    ISSUER
    IRS
    COST
    $0 filing
    LEAD TIME
    Annual; statement to winner at time of payment
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST W-9 on file; withholding capability

    From Instructions for Forms W-2G and 5754 (rev. 01/2026), fetched 14 Sep 2026: for sweepstakes, wagering pools and lotteries, report when winnings meet or exceed the applicable reporting threshold AND are at least 300 times the wager; backup withholding of 24% applies where regular withholding does not and no TIN is supplied. The bingo/slot threshold rose from $1,200 to $2,000 effective 1 January 2026 and thresholds are now annually inflation-indexed under OBBBA (secondary: Holland & Knight, RSM, Baker Tilly). AUDIT CORRECTION, and it reverses the brief's planning conclusion — the brief said 'the 300x rule is decisive for a duel game: a $1 stake winning $100 is 100x and falls below it'. That is WRONG for withholding. The 300x test gates REPORTING; it does NOT gate WITHHOLDING for the category a head-to-head pot most likely falls into. The Instructions state: 'Withhold at the 24% rate if the winnings minus the wager are more than $5,000 and are from: Sweepstakes; Wagering pools; Lotteries...or Sports wagering and other wagering transactions, if the winnings are at least 300 times the amount wagered.' The 300x qualifier attaches ONLY to that final clause — it does not apply to sweepstakes, wagering pools or lotteries, consistent with IRC 3402(q)(3). A FACT//DUEL pot where losers' stakes fund the winner's prize is plausibly a 'wagering pool'. PLANNING RULE: withhold 24% on proceeds (winnings minus wager) over $5,000 regardless of the multiple, unless tax counsel positively characterises the product out of the sweepstakes/wagering-pool/lottery categories. The 24% is unrecoverable from the user once paid out, so the asymmetry is one-sided: over-withholding is a refund problem for the player, under-withholding is an unrecoverable liability for the company.

  • Form 1099-K awareness if you ever become a third-party settlement organisation

    ISSUER
    IRS
    COST
    $0
    LEAD TIME
    Annual
    APPLIES TO
    B

    NEEDS FIRST Determination that you are a TPSO

    OBBBA restored the 1099-K threshold to gross payments exceeding $20,000 AND more than 200 transactions, retroactive to 2022, replacing the $2,500 and $600 phase-ins (secondary: Avalara, TurboTax, Patriot Software). This most likely lands on your PSP rather than on you — but if you settle player-to-player value you may be the TPSO. Confirm with counsel.

  • Unclaimed property / escheatment programme for abandoned user balances

    ISSUER
    State unclaimed-property administrators (NAUPA); most states on a version of RUUPA
    COST
    UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    Annual reporting cycle; dormancy typically 1-5 years depending on state
    APPLIES TO
    B, C (and A only if the coin is ever refundable to cash)

    NEEDS FIRST Balance ledger with last-activity timestamps per user; due-diligence letter capability

    This is the obligation founders miss. A cash-redeemable balance sitting in a dormant account is unclaimed property. Colorado's RUUPA expressly covers stored-value cards including the unused monetary value held by the issuer (secondary: Baker Tilly, EisnerAmper, NAUPA). There is no uniform rule — a state-by-state analysis is required. Two structural facts to raise with counsel: (a) priority is generally to the state of the owner's last known address, falling back to the holder's STATE OF INCORPORATION, so Delaware incorporation pulls all address-unknown balances into Delaware's regime (this priority rule was not verified in this research — flag as UNVERIFIED); (b) a coin that is genuinely non-redeemable in Model A is arguably not 'property' at all, which is another reason the closed-loop design is worth preserving. Design implication: record last-activity timestamps and never silently zero a balance.

  • EU: determine whether you need an EMI or payment institution authorisation, or fit the limited network exclusion

    ISSUER
    National competent authority (e.g. DNB, BaFin, CBI, CSSF); EBA guidelines on the limited network exclusion
    COST
    UNVERIFIED — EMI initial capital and application fees vary by member state
    LEAD TIME
    UNVERIFIED — typically many months
    APPLIES TO
    B (likely required); A (likely excluded)

    NEEDS FIRST EU entity; governance, capital and safeguarding arrangements

    PSD2 Article 3(k) excludes payment instruments usable only in a limited way; secondary commentary is consistent that the exclusion requires NO CASH-OUT, which maps exactly onto the Model A / Model B dividing line. If coins can be converted back to money, you are issuing e-money or providing a payment service and need authorisation plus safeguarding of customer funds. UNVERIFIED: the EBA Guidelines text and the LNE notification thresholds were not fetched. Note the regime is changing — PSD3 and the PSR reportedly reached provisional political agreement on 27 November 2025 and would merge the PI and EMI regimes into one licence, with application around 2027 (secondary, UNVERIFIED). Apply under PSD2/EMD2 now; expect grandfathering.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — register and bond the prize promotion in New York and Florida before it opens

    ISSUER
    New York State Department of State; Florida Department of Agriculture and Consumer Services
    COST
    Filing fee plus a surety bond that must cover the TOTAL advertised value of all prizes, not just the largest prize. Exact fee and bond premium UNVERIFIED — sourced from sweepstakes-practice law-firm commentary, not from the agencies' own fee schedules.
    LEAD TIME
    New York: file 30 days before the promotion starts. Florida: file 7 days before the promotion starts. These are hard pre-opening deadlines — they cannot be cured retroactively.
    APPLIES TO
    C, and any sweepstakes-tier B

    NEEDS FIRST Official rules drafted; total prize value computed; surety bond placed

    Omitted from the brief entirely, despite the brief covering far more exotic obligations like escheatment. The trigger commonly reported is aggregate prize value across the promotion exceeding $5,000. This bites Model C directly and any sweepstakes-tier Model B, is cheap to comply with and expensive to miss, and — critically for a continuously running game — a prize structure that runs perpetually rather than as a discrete 'promotion' with a start date does not fit the registration form's assumptions, which is itself a question for counsel. UNVERIFIED: the $5,000 threshold, the fee amounts, the bond mechanics and the two deadlines all come from secondary law-firm and sweepstakes-administrator commentary (Klein Moynihan Turco, Fasthoff, and similar); no agency page was fetched. Confirm with sweepstakes counsel before relying on any figure here. Note also that several other states impose prize-promotion rules short of registration, and that a paid-entry contest is a different animal from a free-entry sweepstakes — the registration regimes were written for the latter.

Stage 3 — Product controls that must ship before launch 24

  • Decide the product model BEFORE opening any merchant account, and never describe the business to a processor in terms that trip the prohibited list ('stake', 'wager', 'prize', 'cash out', 'escrow', 'winner takes the pot')

    ISSUER
    Internal / Stripe, Adyen, PayPal, Paddle underwriting
    COST
    $0
    LEAD TIME
    immediate
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D

    Underwriting is done on your own copy, so the words the product uses are a real underwriting input. AUDIT CORRECTION — the brief's original claim that FACT//DUEL's live UI 'already uses stake, escrow and winner takes the escrow' is withdrawn as fabricated. Verified by grep across the working tree (node_modules and .git excluded): the phrase 'winner takes the escrow' appears in NO product code — its only occurrences are inside this project's own compliance and research documents (docs/money/*, public/product/compliance/*), which quote the brief itself. 'escrow' appears ZERO times in app/ and components/ (the entire UI layer); it exists only as an internal server-side field in lib/server/room-engine.mjs (r.escrow, r.balances[winner] += r.escrow) and in tests. 'stake' occurs ~55 times in app/ and components/ but overwhelmingly as CSS class names and prop/identifier names (fd-exp-stake-line, stake-label, config.stake); the user-visible strings at those sites are neutral ('Entry · once per match', 'Free entry', 'N coins'). So the exposure is much smaller than the brief asserted and there is no shipped, indexable prohibited-category copy. The remedial action still stands and is cheap: before filing any merchant application, rename the internal escrow/stake concepts and keep user-facing copy in entry/reward language, and make sure the compliance documents themselves are not published at a public URL under the company's own domain where an underwriter would read them as a description of the business. Misdescribing the business to a processor remains a termination-plus-clawback event under every processor agreement, so the fix is to change the product, not the wording.

  • Do NOT attempt Stripe for Models B or C — there is no pre-approval path

    ISSUER
    Stripe
    COST
    n/a
    LEAD TIME
    n/a
    APPLIES TO
    B, C

    Stripe's Prohibited and Restricted Businesses list (last updated 13 May 2026) places in the PROHIBITED column: 'Games of chance including gambling, internet gambling, casino games, sweepstakes and contests, and fantasy sports leagues with a monetary or material prize'; 'Games of skill including video game and mobile game tournaments or competitions, darts, card games, and board games with a monetary or material prize'; and 'Payments of an entry or player fee that promise the entrant or player will win a prize of value'. That third line is a verbatim description of Model C. Restricted-list categories can be pre-approved; PROHIBITED ones cannot. AUDIT CORRECTION — the brief presented 'regardless of local legality' as a verbatim Stripe quotation; that phrase could not be confirmed as text on support.stripe.com's prohibited-business FAQ and is withdrawn as a quote. The SUBSTANCE is sound and is retained unquoted: the FAQ answers 'Internet gambling is legal in my region, so why was my application rejected?' by pointing to Stripe's own processor and financial-partner obligations, i.e. local legality does not by itself make the category acceptable. The adjacent phrase 'games of skill (or chance) with cash prizes, sweepstakes, pay-in auctions' IS verbatim and may be quoted. Do not reproduce the withdrawn phrase inside quotation marks in anything filed or shown to counsel.

  • Do NOT use Paddle or Lemon Squeezy for coin sales in any model

    ISSUER
    Paddle.com Market Ltd (Paddle owns Lemon Squeezy)
    COST
    n/a
    LEAD TIME
    n/a
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C

    Paddle's Acceptable Use Policy (last updated 13 April 2026) prohibits 'Virtual currency or stored value, including but not limited to store credit, gift cards, vouchers' — this kills even Model A's coin purchase, not just the gambling models. It separately prohibits 'lotteries, auctions, contests, sweepstakes, or games of chance', 'Betting-related products, competition/event tickets, or entries', fantasy sports, money transfer services, and marketplaces. Paddle does run a 'Paddle for games' product, but for game and DLC licences, not in-game currency.

  • Understand precisely what merchant-of-record does and does not move

    ISSUER
    Internal / counsel
    COST
    $0
    LEAD TIME
    immediate
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D

    MoR MOVES: the card-scheme merchant relationship and chargeback liability; PCI-DSS merchant scope (the MoR hosts checkout); sales-tax/VAT/GST registration, collection, remittance and invoicing in ~100 jurisdictions; the consumer-contract counterparty and refund obligation. MoR DOES NOT MOVE: gambling or gaming licensing — the licensable act is OFFERING the game, which is you, not the reseller; money transmission — if you hold a user balance and pay it out, that is your activity and no MoR touches it; KYC/AML on payouts; age assurance inside the game; the underlying legality of the stake. Decisive practical point: MoRs are a ONE-WAY pay-in rail. None of Paddle, FastSpring or Lemon Squeezy does player payouts. For Models B and C the payout half — the hard, regulated half — is entirely unserved by MoR.

    PayPal's Acceptable Use Policy prohibits gambling — 'placing, accepting, recording, or registering bets... or otherwise carrying on a game of chance for money, property, or other things of value' — absent pre-approval, and PayPal's help content states approval requires satisfying PayPal that you can block US account holders.   • Apple: use StoreKit in-app purchase for coin sales in Model A and subscriptions in Model D

    Apple
    COST
    AUDIT ADDITION (the brief left the dominant cost line blank).   • Apple: Model B cannot sell its stake currency through IAP — this is a hard block, not a review-risk

    ISSUER
    Apple App Store Review
    COST
    n/a
    LEAD TIME
    n/a
    APPLIES TO
    B

    Guideline 5.3.3 verbatim: 'Apps may not use in-app purchase to purchase credit or currency for use in conjunction with real money gaming of any kind.' Guideline 5.3.4: real-money-gaming apps 'must have necessary licensing and permissions in the locations where the app is used, must be geo-restricted to those locations, and must be free on the App Store.' Combined effect: on iOS a Model B app must be free, licensed, geofenced, and must fund balances OUTSIDE IAP via a web top-up. CONTESTED (audit, possible) — the brief also warned that 'the historic Guideline 4.7.1 real-money-gaming restriction has been removed'. The second half of that warning is right and is retained: current 4.7 covers 'Mini apps, mini games, streaming games, chatbots, plug-ins, and game emulators' and current 4.7.1 concerns privacy compliance, moderation/reporting mechanisms and Guideline 3.1 conformity for digital goods. But the auditor could not confirm that a real-money-gaming restriction ever sat at 4.7.1 — Apple's real-money gaming and sweepstakes rules have lived at 5.3 for years. Treat the claimed citation history as UNVERIFIED and do not repeat it to counsel; the operative point is simply that 5.3, not 4.7, is where real-money gaming lives today.

  • Apple: if Model B or C funds balances off-app, build the web top-up flow and rely on the US-storefront link-out carve-out

    Guideline 3.1.1(a) now states entitlements 'are not required for developers to include buttons, external links, or other calls to action in their United States storefront apps', and that the anti-steering prohibition applies 'In all other storefronts, except for the United States storefront, where this prohibition does not apply'. So a US-storefront app may link out to a web wallet top-up freely; EU and other storefronts need the External Purchase Link Entitlement. India follows the general rule.

  • Google Play: use Google Play Billing for Model A coins and Model D subscriptions

    ISSUER
    Google Play

  • India: assume NO payment rail exists for Models B or C and do not attempt to build one

    COST

    The Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Act, 2025 prohibits all online money games whether of chance, of skill, or a combination, and — per multiple law-firm summaries (Trilegal; PIB press releases) — a dedicated section bars banks, financial institutions and 'any other person facilitating financial transactions' including payment aggregators, gateways, wallet operators and card networks from facilitating any transaction toward payment for an online money gaming service. The section number is reported as section 7 in secondary sources; UNVERIFIED — the statutory text could not be fetched (meity.gov.in and indiacode.nic.in returned 403). AUDIT CORRECTIONS, three of them. (1) SOURCING: the Act number 'Act No. 32 of 2025' and the '22 August 2025' assent date, and the claim that the Rules were notified 22 April 2026, were taken from a Wikipedia article that carries an AI-GENERATED-CONTENT warning. (2) COMMENCEMENT: the same Wikipedia article states commencement is 'TBA — not yet in force'. The brief presented an operative, settled payment-facilitation ban; the in-force status of the relevant sections is UNVERIFIED. (3) LITIGATION: the Act is reported to be under challenge before Indian High Courts and the Supreme Court — the brief mentioned no litigation at all. NET EFFECT ON ADVICE: unchanged and still conservative. The Act's skill-inclusive scope is well corroborated across independent law-firm sources, the direction of travel is one-way, and no Indian payment aggregator will take the criminal-exposure risk while the question is open. Do not attempt an Indian rail for Models B or C. But brief counsel on the commencement question and the pending challenges rather than on a settled ban.

    Note an important structural point: 31 CFR 1010.100(t) defines a BSA 'casino' as an establishment 'duly licensed or authorized to do business as such in the United States' with 'gross annual gaming revenue in excess of $1 million'. An unlicensed sweepstakes-model operator therefore is NOT a BSA casino and does not get the casino rules — it falls into the MSB money-transmitter regime instead, which is the stricter, lower-threshold one.

  • PCI-DSS SAQ A via hosted fields or a hosted checkout

    ISSUER
    PCI Security Standards Council; acquirer enforces
    COST
    $0 for self-assessment; ASV quarterly scan cost UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    Days
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D

    NEEDS FIRST Never let card data touch your DOM or servers

    Use Stripe Elements, Adyen Drop-in/Components or an equivalent iframe/hosted-field integration and you qualify for SAQ A rather than SAQ A-EP or D. Under PCI DSS v4.0.1, effective 1 April 2025, the SAQ A eligibility criteria now require the merchant to confirm its site 'is not susceptible to attacks from scripts that could affect the merchant's e-commerce system(s)'. CONTESTED (audit, possible) — the brief said Requirements 6.4.3 (authorise and assure integrity of every script on the payment page) and 11.6.1 (change-and-tamper detection on the payment page), though removed from the SAQ A checklist itself, 'are named as the techniques by which you demonstrate eligibility', which reads as though a script-inventory/SRI/CSP programme is THE route. The PCI SSC FAQ presents them as ONE route: a merchant may either deploy those techniques itself OR obtain confirmation from its payment processor that the processor's solution includes such protections. Ask Stripe or Adyen for that confirmation in writing before building the programme yourself. TWO SCOPE FACTS THE BRIEF OMITTED, both audit additions: (a) the new criteria apply to merchants using EMBEDDED payment forms (iframes) — which is exactly the integration pattern the brief recommends; (b) a FULL REDIRECT or fully hosted payment page sits outside the new criteria entirely (see the separate checklist item on this). Given that FACT//DUEL is a heavily scripted React/three.js app in the classic Magecart target profile, the redirect option is the cheap architectural answer and should be evaluated first. If you build your own card form you drop to SAQ A-EP and the burden multiplies.

  • Separate the money ledger from the game ledger in the data model before any of this

    ISSUER
    Internal architecture
    COST
    Engineering only
    LEAD TIME
    Design decision — cheapest now, very expensive later
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D

    Today all profile state is in IndexedDB with no server identity. Whatever model is chosen, keep three ledgers physically distinct: (1) purchased value, (2) earned/granted value, (3) prize/withdrawable value. Never let them commingle into one 'coins' integer. Regulators, processors and auditors all ask the same question — 'can purchased value become withdrawable value?' — and a single-integer wallet cannot answer 'no'. Dual-currency separation is also the only way to keep Model A defensible if you later bolt on a sweepstakes tier.

  • EU: AML obligations as a provider of gambling services

    ISSUER
    Regulation (EU) 2024/1624 (AMLR); AMLA; national FIUs
    COST
    UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    UNVERIFIED
    APPLIES TO
    B, C (if characterised as gambling in the member state)

    NEEDS FIRST Member-state gambling characterisation

    AUDIT ADDITION — the sequencing fact the brief omitted: Regulation (EU) 2024/1624 (AMLR) does NOT apply immediately. It applies across all 27 member states from 10 JULY 2027 (corroborated across several independent practitioner sources: Deloitte, Bird & Bird, and EU AML-package readiness commentary; not fetched from EUR-Lex, which was unreachable across four attempts, so treat the date as high-confidence secondary rather than primary-verified). Until that date the member state's existing AMLD transposition governs, so for a 2026-2027 launch this is a ROADMAP item, not a launch blocker — but the national AMLD regime IS a launch blocker and must be scoped separately. ALSO AUDIT-ADDED and directly decision-relevant to Model C: AMLR is reported to introduce the first EU-level definition of 'gambling service', described in secondary sources as a service involving 'wagering a stake with monetary value in games of chance, INCLUDING THOSE WITH AN ELEMENT OF SKILL', at Article 2(1)(12). If that is right, the EU AML perimeter expressly reaches skill games and the Model C 'it is skill, not gambling' argument does not remove the AML obligation even where it removes the gambling licence. The article number and the exact wording are UNVERIFIED against EUR-Lex — counsel must confirm from primary text. What counsel must still pin down: (1) the article listing providers of gambling services as obliged entities; (2) the CDD trigger threshold for wagering a stake and for collecting winnings; (3) whether the member state has exercised any low-risk exemption; (4) the national AMLD position that governs until 10 July 2027. Do not act on remembered figures for any of these.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — understand UIGEA (31 U.S.C. 5361-5367) and Regulation GG as the federal law that makes the rail refuse, not a risk appetite you can negotiate

    ISSUER
    Unlawful Internet Gambling Enforcement Act of 2006; Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System and US Treasury (Regulation GG)
    COST
    $0 to understand; the compliance cost lands on your acquirer and is priced into their refusal
    LEAD TIME
    immediate
    APPLIES TO
    B, C (and the reason A and D must stay visibly clear of the category)

    The single largest omission in the original brief. UIGEA is codified at 31 U.S.C. 5361-5367 (enacted 13 October 2006). It is implemented by Regulation GG — 12 CFR Part 233 (Federal Reserve) and 31 CFR Part 132 (Treasury), published 18 November 2008 — which requires designated payment systems (ACH, card systems, checks, wire transfer, and money transmitting businesses) to establish written policies and procedures to identify and BLOCK 'restricted transactions' connected to unlawful internet gambling. Under 31 U.S.C. 5367 a financial transaction provider can be liable in the same manner as the internet gambling business itself where it has actual knowledge and control of bets and wagers, and courts have rejected the argument that UIGEA does not reach third-party payment processors. WHY THIS CHANGES THE FOUNDER'S MENTAL MODEL: the brief explained Stripe's and Adyen's category lists as commercial policy, which invites the assumption that a good enough story, a licence, or a higher rate will move them. It will not. The lists exist because a federal statute imposes a coding-and-blocking mandate on the rail. The corollary that actually matters strategically: UIGEA bites on UNLAWFUL internet gambling, defined by reference to the underlying state or federal law — so if Model C is genuinely lawful skill in a given state, UIGEA does not reach it there, and the underlying legality analysis is therefore a PAYMENTS prerequisite, not a separate workstream (see the skill-versus-chance item below). VERIFIED via search against FDIC and Cornell LII materials; the statutory and CFR citations were not fetched from eCFR (blocked from this environment) — confirm the Part numbers before filing.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — model chargeback and friendly-fraud exposure on coin purchases, and instrument against Visa VDMP and Mastercard excessive-chargeback thresholds

    ISSUER
    Visa Dispute Monitoring Program; Mastercard Excessive Chargeback Program; your acquirer
    COST
    Per-dispute fee plus fines once in a monitoring programme — all amounts UNVERIFIED; get the current thresholds and fee schedule from the prospective acquirer
    LEAD TIME
    Ongoing from first transaction
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D (sharpest on A)

    NEEDS FIRST Dispute instrumentation; delivery evidence; refund policy

    This is a realistic way Model A — the model the brief graded unconditionally lawful and fully banked — LOSES its processor, and the brief did not mention it. Digital goods with instant delivery and no shippable proof draw elevated dispute rates: there is no tracking number, no signature, and 'I did not authorise this' from a parent whose child bought coins is both common and hard to rebut. Card-network monitoring programmes impose escalating fines and can force a merchant off the rail entirely; a MATCH/TMF listing follows termination and blacklists the principals, not just the company. Concrete mitigations to build before launch, not after: clear billing descriptor; receipt email on every purchase; in-app purchase history the user can see; a generous self-serve refund path (cheaper than a dispute); device and IP evidence retained per transaction; and store-level parental-consent handling in Texas, Utah and Louisiana, which doubles as friendly-fraud defence. UNVERIFIED: current VDMP and Mastercard ECP ratio and count thresholds and their fine schedules were not researched.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — evaluate a full-redirect or fully hosted checkout FIRST, because it sits outside the new SAQ A script-attack eligibility criteria entirely

    ISSUER
    PCI Security Standards Council; acquirer enforces
    COST
    Engineering only; likely cheaper than an ongoing script-integrity programme
    LEAD TIME
    Days
    APPLIES TO
    A, B, C, D

    The obvious architectural answer the brief never offered. The PCI SSC FAQ limits the new SAQ A script-attack eligibility criteria to merchants using EMBEDDED payment forms (iframes). A merchant that fully redirects the browser to the processor's own hosted payment page is outside those criteria. Given the brief's own finding that FACT//DUEL is a script-heavy React/three.js single-page app in exactly the Magecart target profile, redirecting to Stripe Checkout or Adyen's hosted page removes the entire script-inventory / SRI / CSP burden from the compliance perimeter rather than managing it. TRADE-OFF to weigh honestly: a redirect costs conversion and breaks the in-app feel, which is why teams reach for embedded fields by default. The point is that the choice should be made deliberately with the PCI cost priced in, not defaulted into. UNVERIFIED: the FAQ was read by the auditor, not fetched in this pass; confirm the iframe-only scoping with your QSA or acquirer before relying on it.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — do the state-by-state skill-versus-chance analysis for paid-entry contests BEFORE the payments workstream, because it determines the payments outcome

    ISSUER
    State gambling statutes and AG opinions; gambling counsel
    COST
    Legal opinion — UNVERIFIED
    LEAD TIME
    Weeks to months
    APPLIES TO
    B, C

    NEEDS FIRST Fixed product design (entry fee, prize source, prize determination)

    The brief walled this off into 'the gambling-law layer'. It cannot be cleanly walled off. Several US states treat ANY paid-entry cash-prize contest as illegal gambling regardless of whether skill predominates, because their statutes use a consideration-plus-prize-plus-chance test in which any chance element is enough, or because they read an entry fee as consideration per se. Two payments consequences follow directly. (1) UIGEA exposure is defined by reference to the underlying state law, so the states where a paid-entry skill contest is unlawful are exactly the states where card and ACH transactions become 'restricted transactions' a designated payment system must block. (2) Acquirer acceptance and any Adyen-style waiver turn on a demonstration of lawfulness, which is this opinion. So the geo-blocking map for Model C is an output of this analysis, and the checkout cannot be built until it exists. UNVERIFIED: no state-level determination is asserted here; that work sits in the US state lane and this item exists to record that the payments plan is blocked on it.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — before relying on any 'licensed operator' acquirer route for Model C, establish whether a skill-contest licence exists to be obtained at all

    ISSUER
    Gambling counsel in each target market; Adyen and specialist-acquirer underwriting
    COST
    $0 to ask; a legal opinion per market otherwise
    LEAD TIME
    Weeks
    APPLIES TO
    C

    The brief listed 'gambling or skill-gaming licence in each target market' as the prerequisite for Adyen's waiver AND for every specialist acquirer, then described Adyen as 'more workable' for Model C. Those two statements may be incompatible. In the UK, a paid-entry prize competition decided by genuine skill, knowledge or judgement is lawful WITHOUT a Gambling Commission licence and sits outside the licensing regime — meaning there is no licence to obtain, so a prerequisite expressed as 'hold a licence' is unsatisfiable rather than merely slow. Several EU member states are reported to be similar for pure skill contests, though this varies sharply and was not verified state by state. If that is right in a given market, the 'Adyen route' for Model C is illusory and the payments verdict for Model C moves materially closer to Model B's — which is the change the auditor flagged as most likely to alter the plan. THE ACTION IS CHEAP AND SHOULD HAPPEN FIRST: ask Adyen, and one specialist acquirer, in writing, what evidence of lawfulness they accept for a paid-entry skill contest in a jurisdiction that issues no licence for it. A written 'a counsel opinion suffices' answer unlocks the route; silence or 'licence required' closes it. UNVERIFIED: the UK position is from practitioner commentary on the Gambling Act 2005, not from Gambling Commission primary text; no EU member-state position is asserted.

  • AUDIT ADDITION — price store commission into the unit economics before choosing between the in-app and web funding path

    ISSUER
    Internal / finance
    COST

    The auditor's point, and it is a fair one: the brief priced every marginal vendor fee — $1.50 per identity check, 2.9% + $0.30 per card charge — while leaving blank the single largest cost line in the entire document for the two models it clears for launch. Store commission is roughly ten times the card fee. Three consequences for the plan. (2) The web top-up path is not merely a Model B compliance workaround; for Model A and D it is a margin decision, which is why Apple's US-storefront link-out carve-out and Play's EEA and US alternative-billing options are commercially material. (3) Merchant-of-record fees for Model D must be compared against store commission, not against raw Stripe pricing, because the realistic alternative to Paddle is Apple, not a bare card rail.
