Critical path

  1. 01Freeze the model decision to A + D, kill B permanently, and shelve C. Freeze the rake/prize structure in writing at the same time (operator-funded fixed announced prize vs commission on a player-funded pot).

    Gate: Founder sign-off only. No external approval.

    Blocks: Everything. Until the rake structure is frozen, every counsel instruction in every region is answering the wrong question, and the Germany/Austria/Netherlands out-of-scope analysis and the US money-transmission analysis cannot be run at all.

  2. 02Build the backend foundation: accounts, server authority, and two HARD-SEPARATED ledgers — purchased currency that is spend-only and structurally incapable of being staked, and earned currency that is stakeable and worthless. Add FIFO coin-lot tracking (spent vs unspent), last-activity timestamps, and separation of the money ledger from the game ledger.

    Gate: Engineering only. No regulator, no approval.

    Blocks: All monetisation in all three regions. Also blocks geofencing, age gating, KYC, EU VAT location evidence, state escheat reporting, the EU 14-day refund path, and prevention of the stake-then-refund free-roll. Near-impossible to retrofit.

  3. 03Remediate the CURRENT FREE PRODUCT: COPPA programme (neutral age screen, direct notice, verifiable parental consent for under-13s, retention/deletion policy), preserve and strengthen the existing bot disclosure, and scope the India DPDP child-data and 2026 Rules social-game duties.

    Gate: None — COPPA has been in full force since 22 April 2026 and the India duties attach to a free product with Indian users today.

    Blocks: Nothing downstream, which is exactly why it gets skipped. It is live, unhedged exposure on the build that is already shipping.

  4. 04Obtain WRITTEN category acceptance from Stripe for closed-loop, non-redeemable coin sales and for the subscription, naming the business model accurately. Do not use Paddle or Lemon Squeezy for coin sales in any model.

    Gate: Stripe underwriting response in writing.

    Blocks: The US and EU Model A checkout. Do not infer acceptance from absence on a prohibited list — Adyen and PayPal virtual-currency acceptance is UNVERIFIED and Paddle prohibits stored value outright.

  5. 05Launch D + A in the USA. Ship ROSCA-compliant subscription flow, state auto-renewal compliance (California ARL in force, Virginia 1 Jul 2026, NYC 1 Oct 2026), single-step purchase confirmation with plain-language coin pricing, FTC dark-pattern review of the currency-conversion flow, OFAC screening, and a documented escheat/unclaimed-property position.

    Gate: No licence and no approval exist for this path. Store review time is UNVERIFIED.

    Blocks: First revenue. This is the earliest revenue available to the company and nothing legal stands in front of it.

  6. 06Launch D + A in the EU. Register for non-Union VAT OSS BEFORE the first EU sale; capture two pieces of non-contradictory location evidence per sale; appoint a GDPR Art 27 representative (and a DSA Art 13 representative once accounts ship) and run an Art 35 DPIA; put a CRD Art 8(2) 'order with obligation to pay' button on EVERY purchase surface; comply with the CPC virtual-currency principles; publish e-Commerce Directive provider identification.

    Gate: VAT OSS registration lead time UNVERIFIED — this is the only external wait on the A/D path and must be started early. No gambling licence needed.

    Blocks: EU revenue. Note the CRD Art 8(2) button is the cheapest fix in this document: without it the consumer is not bound by the contract and revenue reverses. Hard rule throughout: NO randomised paid rewards anywhere in the EU (Belgium criminal exposure).

  7. 07Launch D and PURE A in India. Register for GST (and OIDAR from the first rupee if supplied from outside India); build DPDP child-data compliance; ship the 2026 Rules social-game safety duties; scrub all advertising and app-store copy of money-game framing. Obtain a written Indian counsel opinion on the s.2(1)(j) construction BEFORE enabling any India purchase flow.

    Gate: Written Indian counsel opinion on s.2(1)(j) — a hard gate, because the exposure is criminal and unquantified rather than a fine. Consider whether a voluntary OGAI determination is worth filing, noting the UNVERIFIED report that registration triggers India data residency.

    Blocks: India revenue. Absolute limits inside A that no opinion can cure: coins must never buy entry to a prize of value, and there must be no transferability, gifting, secondary market or account trading.

  8. 08ONLY AFTER A/D REVENUE EXISTS: run the Model C feasibility gate. Three written answers, in this order — (1) a per-state US gaming-counsel opinion covering skill/chance, the money-transmission threshold question, tribal compacts, player-side liability, escheat and AMOE; (2) a written answer from Adyen underwriting on whether its 'LEGAL gambling or Game of Skill' category requires a licence that does not exist for pure skill contests; (3) a member-state answer on whether ANY paid-entry skill-contest licence exists in the UK or any EU state, plus the Germany s.33d GewO online-reach question.

    Gate: All three answers in writing. Published data point for the US opinion: $15,000 advertised for a 51-jurisdiction skill-gaming opinion; full-service firms materially more. One firm reportedly reached ZERO unconditionally cleared states.

    Blocks: Any Model C engineering. Do not build first and clear later — the payments workstream cannot even begin until the licensing workstream ends, because every specialist acquirer requires the licence first.

  9. 09If Model C proceeds: build a SEPARATE contest engine — fixed operator-funded prize announced before entries open, identical question sets, no randomised draw, no randomised matchmaking, no chance-introducing timer, no withdrawable balance, every opponent a disclosed human or disclosed bot. Geofence ~11 US states plus France; sequence the geofence against effective dates.

    Gate: Oklahoma 1 Nov 2026 is the only listed prohibition not yet operative — Class C2 felony, no wind-down, vendor liability. Do not stage into that window. Withhold 24% over $5,000 of proceeds regardless of multiple. Settle the Marchetti/Grosso privilege question with counsel BEFORE any Form 11-C filing, since filing at the $500 unauthorised rate is a signed admission.

    Blocks: Model C launch. Note the existing Expedition loop cannot be monetised directly — it is the worst-case fact pattern under every material-element test.

  10. 10Never: Model B, in any region, under any structure.

    Gate: No gate exists that opens it at seed stage.

    Blocks: N/A — this is the recommendation to stop, not a sequenced step.

Open questions for counsel

Every unverified fact on this page, turned into something answerable by number. The honest thing to do with a fact you cannot confirm is to bill someone to confirm it.

  1. Q1Does German s.33d GewO apply to a purely online offering? The provision is verified at gesetze-im-internet.de; its online reach is not. This is the single most important open question in the EU lane and it sits in the market the original brief recommended launching in first. No fee or lead time is known for the trade Erlaubnis or the BKA clearance certificate.
  2. Q2Is a 5/7/10-second timed trivia duel over a randomly drawn question set a 'game of chance with an element of skill'? No case law, no guidance, anywhere. Under AMLD4 Art 3(14) this is a PRESENT-TENSE question, not a 2027 one, and the single answer decides EU gambling licensing, AML obliged-entity status and VAT Art 135(1)(i) exemption simultaneously. The identical question decides 'game subject to chance' under UIGEA s.5362(1)(A) and the material-element tests in the US states. Instruct it once, covering all limbs.
  3. Q3How is PROGA s.2(1)(j) construed for purchased, non-cashable, non-transferable coins staked player-v-player in India? No OGAI determination, no judgment, counsel differ, and the exposure is criminal and unquantified rather than a fine. This is the central commercial question for any India duel monetisation.
  4. Q4Which EU member states have exercised the AMLD4 Art 2(2) low-risk gambling exemption, and for which categories? Now the most operationally load-bearing unknown for Model C. Requires market-by-market enquiry; the corresponding AMLR article preserving the option is also UNVERIFIED.
  5. Q5Does ANY licence exist for a pure paid-entry skill contest in the UK or any EU member state? This determines whether Model C has a mainstream EU payment rail at all, and whether the Adyen 'LEGAL gambling or Game of Skill' route is real or illusory. The UK position rests on practitioner commentary, not primary text. One written question to Adyen underwriting settles the payments half.
  6. Q6What is the current scope of the Marchetti/Grosso Fifth Amendment privilege as applied to 26 U.S.C. ss.4411/4412 today? Must be settled before ANY Form 11-C filing, because filing at the $500 unauthorised rate is a signed admission of accepting wagers not authorised under state law.
  7. Q7Does the CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act game-platform / merchant-rewards exclusion reach a closed-loop coin? The exact wording is the single highest-value item for US counsel, because it determines whether Model A needs any state licence at all. Also unresolved: the MTMA adopter count ('31 enacted' and 'Massachusetts the 26th adopter' cannot both be true).
  8. Q8Is a Model C entry fee a 'wager' for IRS purposes — W-2G or 1099-MISC Box 3? Needs a tax opinion, not a search. Pull the original text of Rev. Rul. 57-521 first; it is currently known only through the quotation inside AM 2020-009.
  9. Q9Has Skillz v. Papaya been appealed, and what is its Second Circuit status? Not checked. Nothing in any file should describe it as settled, and whether the disgorgement theory and the Lanham Act framing survive appeal is the most decision-relevant open item for anyone sizing bot-disclosure risk.
  10. Q10Do the India 2026 Rules in fact impose age verification, parental controls, time limits, fair-play monitoring and grievance redressal on ONLINE SOCIAL GAMES? Asserted by the audit but not corroborable from SCC Online, Bar & Bench, Legal500 or ipleaders. High cost impact on Models A and D AND on the live free build. Related: does registration trigger India data residency, which would be an undisclosed cost of filing a voluntary determination? Every 2026 rule number remains indicative — the gazette text was never obtained and sources actively conflict.
  11. Q11Does Vermont 13 V.S.A. s.2143b ('not required to venture money or other valuable things') bar paid-entry contests entirely? Both direct fetches failed. This is the single highest-value unverified item in the US state lane — if correct, Vermont is an absolute geofence for Model C.
  12. Q12Do tribal compact provisions in CA, FL, OK and CT reach a non-casino skill product? Entirely unverified; no pass was made. Oklahoma's S.B. 1589 carries tribal exclusivity and a Class C2 felony with no wind-down, effective 1 Nov 2026.
  13. Q13Confirm from enrolled bills, not secondary reporting: Indiana H.B. 1052 / P.L. 153-2026, Iowa S.F. 2289, Maine L.D. 2007, Tennessee S.B. 2136, New Jersey A5447, and the Louisiana instrument (SB 181 of 2025 R.S. was VETOED 12 June 2025 and is not law; secondary reporting points to 2026 acts, reported HB 53 / HB 883, with a 1 August effective date — the date may be right but the instrument was wrong). California AB 831, New York S5935A and Oklahoma S.B. 1589 all checked out exactly. No date from this lane should enter a launch timeline before re-verification.
  14. Q14Has PSD3/PSR in fact been published in the Official Journal as at September 2026, and what is the exact transitional article governing the 24/30-month EMI re-application after EMD2's repeal? Only relevant if Model B is ever revisited, but it would make any 2027 e-money licence a wasting asset.
  15. Q15Confirm current Apple s.5.3 and Google Play real-money gaming policy text directly — it is UNVERIFIED throughout, and store policy stops more products than federal law does. Confirm it BEFORE spending on legal opinions. Also unverified: Google Play's base service fee entirely, and Apple's EU DMA 13% rate and US external-link commission, both in litigation flux.
  16. Q16Does a genuine alternative method of entry (AMOE) open any state that paid entry closes? Unverified, no consideration analysis performed. The layer that would actually govern an AMOE redesign — the Prize Competitions Act, 1955 and state prize-competition/lottery law in India, and US state prize-promotion registration — was absent from the original briefs entirely. Note NY prize-promotion registration is a hard 30-day pre-opening deadline with a bond equal to total prize value and no retroactive cure, and Florida is reported at 7 days.
  17. Q17Whether a head-to-head trivia duel is 'a game subject to chance' within 31 U.S.C. § 5362(1)(A). No federal case law located on online knowledge games. The question-draw, matchmaking and 5/7/10-second reaction race all introduce chance elements; a court could go either way. Needs a reasoned counsel opinion, not a guess. This is the same factual question that decides whether the Rev. Rul. 57-521 excise position holds — the two stand or fall together.

    United States

  18. Q18CORRECTED FROM 'UNTESTED': whether a fixed, pre-announced, operator-funded prize escapes 'wagering pool ... conducted for profit' under 26 U.S.C. § 4421(1)(B) is NOT unsupported. Rev. Rul. 57-521, 1957-2 C.B. 779 is directly on point and favourable, and AM 2020-009 endorses it. What remains genuinely uncertain is (a) whether FACT//DUEL's chance elements take it outside the ruling, and (b) whether the IRS would apply the ruling to a modern app. I have NOT read Rev. Rul. 57-521 in its original form — only the extensive quotation of it inside AM 2020-009 via the audit. Pull the ruling text before relying on it.

    United States

January 2026) and the Federal Register listing of 17 April 2026, but the full Federal Register document was not read and the code sections beyond W-2G were not independently confirmed. Confirm before configuring tax reporting.

    United States

Its existence is confirmed; the brief asserted it 'gives limited relief for data collected solely to run age-verification technology' without stating what the relief is conditioned on. Read it in full before designing the age gate.

  37. Q37WHITE v. CUOMO VOTE SPLIT IS UNVERIFIED. Gibson Dunn describes a majority by Chief Judge DiFiore and a dissent by Judge Wilson joined by two others, which is consistent with 4-3 on a seven-judge court, but the opinion itself was not read (Justia, leagle, FindLaw and the NY Courts reporter were unreachable). Do not state the split. Separately and more importantly, the holding is confined to NY Constitution art. I s.9 and to interactive fantasy sports contests that the legislature had authorized in Racing Law art. 14 and exempted from Penal Law art. 225 — it is not clearance for an unauthorized paid-entry trivia contest under Penal Law s.225.00(1).

    United States

  38. Q38MONTANA 'CONTEST OF SKILL' — RESOLVED AGAINST THE BRIEF, AND A REPLACEMENT CITE IS STILL MISSING. Verified 14 Sep 2026 at mca.legmt.gov: MCA 23-5-110 is 'Public policy of state concerning gambling' and defines nothing; MCA 23-5-112 defines 44 terms and 'contest of skill' is not among them; MCA 23-5-112(14)(a) defines gambling on a chance basis. Several promotions-law guides (and one AI-generated blog) attribute a 'contest of skill' definition with a paid-entry disqualifier to 23-5-112, but that text is NOT in the current section. It may exist in an earlier version of the code or in another title. UNVERIFIED — do not cite any Montana 'contest of skill' definition until a primary source is produced. Montana's Model B exposure rests on S.B. 555 alone, and Montana's Model C status is now UNRESOLVED rather than barred.

    United States

  101. Q101THE CENTRAL UNCERTAINTY: the construction of s.2(1)(j) as applied to purchased, non-cashable, non-transferable coins staked player-v-player. s.2(1)(j) reads "anything recognised as equivalent or convertible to money and includes credits, coins, token or objects ... which is purchased by paying money directly or by indirect means or as part of, or in relation to, an online game". CONJUNCTIVE READING (adopted throughout this corrected lane): the chapeau requirement of convertibility to money gates the whole definition, so non-convertible coins are not "other stakes" whether purchased or free. AGGRESSIVE READING: "which is purchased" is a self-sufficient inclusive limb, and "or as part of, or in relation to, an online game" could even capture coins merely acquired in-game. No determination and no judgment resolves this. The audit correctly found the prior version applied the two readings inconsistently depending on which produced the more alarming answer.

    India (Union of India

  102. Q102Whether the winning limb of s.2(1)(g) requires MONETARY enrichment. The verified text reads "in expectation of winning which entails monetary AND other enrichment in return of money or other stakes" — the conjunction is "and" (verified verbatim against Bar & Bench's quotation of the section; NOT checked against the gazette). If "and" is conjunctive, monetary enrichment is necessary and winning further non-monetary in-game credit alone does not satisfy the limb. The prior version relied on a disjunctive reading without disclosing the conjunction. UNRESOLVED.

    India (Union of India

  103. Q103I could NOT obtain the gazette text of the Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Rules, 2026. The G.S.R. number is UNVERIFIED (the PIB backgrounder gives the Gazette of India reference CG-DL-E-22042026-271974, dated 22 April 2026). EVERY rule number cited for the 2026 Rules is INDICATIVE ONLY and the sources conflict: Legal500 and gaganlegal place the five-factor determination test at rule 9 (gaganlegal: rule 8 determination, 9 factors, 10 procedure, 11 validity, 12 registration, 13 e-sports, 14 certificate validity), while Bar & Bench assigns rule 9 to permissible e-sports payments and puts mandatory e-sports registration at rule 12(1)(b), and ipleaders puts registration across rules 12-19 with grievance in Part V. Legal500 separately cites rule 19 (financial-intermediary verification), rule 20 (user grievance appeals) and rule 26 (public register). By contrast, EVERY section number and quotation from the ACT itself is verified against the MeitY-published primary text of Act No. 32 of 2025.

    India (Union of India

  104. Q104Whether the 2026 Rules impose age verification, parental controls, time limits, fair-play monitoring and grievance redressal on ONLINE SOCIAL GAMES — i.e. on the lawful models and the current free product. Asserted by the audit; I could NOT corroborate the scope from SCC Online, Bar & Bench, Legal500 or ipleaders, each of which summarises the money-game prohibition and registration architecture without enumerating affirmative duties for permitted categories. HIGH decision relevance: verify against the gazette text before launch.

    India (Union of India

  105. Q105Whether registration triggers an India DATA-RESIDENCY obligation (retention of traffic data, metadata and related information on computer resources located in India) for registered online social games and e-sports. Asserted by the audit; NOT corroborated here. If true it is a direct cost of this lane's own recommendation to file for a voluntary determination.

    India (Union of India

  106. Q106The prior version's claim that the final 2026 Rules "dropped the Grievance Appellate Committee tier" is WITHDRAWN as fabricated — contradicted by Mondaq ("30-day timelines for Authority and Appellate Authority decisions") and Bar & Bench (appeal to the Secretary, MeitY; 30 days to file, 30 for disposal). What remains genuinely uncertain is the rule number, the precise scope of appealable decisions, and whether the same route covers a cessation direction and a blocking order as well as a classification determination.

    India (Union of India

  107. Q107OGAI registration and determination FEES: UNVERIFIED. Secondary sources say the fee is published on the OGAI portal; no amount retrieved, and ogai.gov.in returned a 502 from this environment.

    India (Union of India

  108. Q108Whether a provider with NO Indian presence can file a determination application at all, and what any point-of-contact / India-resident-officer requirement does to that: UNVERIFIED (audit addition). Also unquantified: the exposure created by putting a named foreign operator in front of the regulator with a high-scrutiny design.

    India (Union of India

  109. Q109LEAD TIMES for incorporation, GST/OIDAR registration and PA-CB onboarding: UNVERIFIED. The only lead time stated with confidence is the 90-day determination/registration target, and that clock resets on each request for further information.

    India (Union of India

  110. Q110Whether the Central Government has notified ANY category of online social game into mandatory registration: reported as none, but UNVERIFIED as at 14 September 2026. The single item most likely to change; re-check immediately before an India launch.

  111. Q111Whether OGAI has issued any determination touching cosmetic-only in-app purchases, purchased virtual currency or loot boxes: NONE found. The position for Model A rests on statutory construction and commentary, not regulator practice.

    India (Union of India

  112. Q112The RBI payment-aggregator framework. The prior version's citation — "Reserve Bank of India (Regulation of Payment Aggregators) Directions, 2025, dated 15 September 2025", three categories with an "Outward Collection Account" and a ₹25 lakh per-transaction cap — is WITHDRAWN. The words "Regulation of" and the 15 September 2025 date could not be confirmed on RBI's Master Directions or notifications pages; "Outward Collection Account" is not RBI terminology (the PA-CB framework uses the Import Collection Account for Indians paying a foreign merchant and the Export Collection Account for the reverse); and the ₹25 lakh figure derives from the October 2023 PA-CB circular's per-unit import cap with survival into the 2025 consolidation UNVERIFIED. Do not plan payment architecture against those labels or that cap.

    India (Union of India

  113. Q113The DPDP Rules 2025 gazette reference. The prior version cited "G.S.R. 846(E), 13 November 2025" three times. CONTESTED: the G.S.R. number could not be corroborated and Wikipedia gives 14 November 2025 for notification. Cite the Rules by name only until checked against the gazette. Similarly UNVERIFIED: the specific rule numbers for verification mechanics (reported rule 10) and Significant Data Fiduciary obligations (reported rule 13), and the Fourth Schedule location of the s.9(4) exemptions.

    India (Union of India

  114. Q114Whether PROG Act s.14 permits blocking NOTWITHSTANDING s.69A of the IT Act 2000, or instead operates through the s.69A procedure and the 2009 Blocking Rules. CONTESTED and material: it decides whether the s.69A procedural safeguards attach.

    India (Union of India

  115. Q115The Act's commencement date. The MeitY-published compilation carries an internal inconsistency: the header records "President's assent & Gazette Notification, dated 22.08.2025", while footnote 1 reads "dated 22.9.2025". Every other source, including PIB, gives 22 August 2025 for assent; treated as a typographical error. s.1(3) left commencement to notification and the Act was brought into force on 1 May 2026 by notifications issued 22 April 2026.

    India (Union of India

  116. Q116Supreme Court status. Head Digital Works v Union of India, T.C.(C) No. 133/2025 was pending as at the last case-tracker update retrievable (hearing listed 5 August 2026). Whether judgment has since been delivered or reserved could not be confirmed. Re-check before relying on any of this.

    India (Union of India

  117. Q117The reported Supreme Court decision of 27 May 2026 upholding 28% GST on online gaming rests on a SINGLE secondary source and could not be corroborated. The separate 40% GST slab for online money gaming from 22 September 2025 (56th GST Council) is better corroborated but is commercially moot given the s.5 ban.

    India (Union of India

  118. Q118The precise GST rate and SAC classification for cosmetic in-app purchases as distinct from subscriptions: reported at 18% as an online service / OIDAR supply; exact classification UNVERIFIED.

    India (Union of India

    India (Union of India

  121.   124. Q124The State rows in this lane are an audit addition and their State-enactment details are UNVERIFIED. PROGA is central and governs uniformly, so the model verdicts do not turn on them; what is unverified is the residual question of whether any ADDITIONAL (as opposed to inconsistent, and therefore overridden) State duty survives s.18, and whether historic State skill-game licensing regimes (Nagaland, Sikkim, Meghalaya) retain any effect. None of them creates a route to market for Model B or C.

  125. Q125EU AML Regulation (EU) 2024/1624: no article number, EUR customer-due-diligence threshold for gambling, member-state low-risk exemption, or date of application is asserted. eur-lex.europa.eu returned empty responses across four separate fetch attempts (HTML, PDF, CELEX and ELI URL forms) and the environment's proxy would not relay it. Everything about AMLR in this report is a statement of what counsel must find, not a finding.

    USA, EU and India

  126. Q126India, Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Act 2025: the section numbers (reported as sections 5, 6 and 7 for the offering, advertising and financial-transaction prohibitions), the penalty amounts (reported as up to 3 years imprisonment and INR 1 crore) and the commencement date all come from secondary sources — Trilegal, PIB press releases and Wikipedia. meity.gov.in and indiacode.nic.in both returned HTTP 403, and ogai.gov.in was refused by the proxy gateway. The substance — a total ban on online money games including skill games, plus a payment-facilitation prohibition — is corroborated across independent sources and is safe to plan around; the citations are not safe to file.

    USA, EU and India

  127. Q12731 CFR 1010.311 and 1010.306 (Currency Transaction Report threshold and filing deadline) were not verified. ecfr.gov redirects to an unblock page from this environment. The $10,000 figure is well known but is not asserted here as verified, and CTR applicability to a card-funded online game is genuinely doubtful since CTRs attach to currency.

    USA, EU and India

  128. Q12831 CFR 1010.100(ff)(8): the $1,000-per-person-per-day de minimis that applies to most MSB categories and its reported non-application to money transmitters could not be confirmed. The Cornell fetch did not surface it. If a money transmitter has no de minimis — which is the conventional understanding — then even a single small redeemable payout creates MSB status, which is a material planning fact. Confirm before relying on it.

    USA, EU and India

The reported first EU-level definition of 'gambling service' as covering games of chance 'including those with an element of skill', attributed in secondary sources to Article 2(1)(12), is UNVERIFIED as to both the article number and the wording, and is decision-critical for Model C.     USA, EU and India

    USA, EU and India

  149. Q149AUDIT NOTE on Stripe — the phrase 'regardless of local legality' is withdrawn as a quotation; it could not be confirmed as text on Stripe's prohibited-business FAQ. The substance (local legality does not by itself make the category acceptable, because Stripe's own processor and financial-partner obligations bind) is sound and is retained unquoted. The adjacent phrase 'games of skill (or chance) with cash prizes, sweepstakes, pay-in auctions' IS verbatim and may be quoted.

    USA, EU and India

  150. Q150AUDIT NOTE on Apple Guideline history — the brief's warning about a 'historic Guideline 4.7.1 real-money-gaming restriction' that has since been removed could not be corroborated; Apple's real-money gaming and sweepstakes rules appear to have lived at 5.3 throughout. Current 4.7 covers mini apps, mini games, streaming games, chatbots, plug-ins and emulators, and current 4.7.1 concerns privacy, moderation and Guideline 3.1 conformity. Do not repeat the claimed citation history to counsel.

    USA, EU and India
